Abstract: AI models of the power grid must capture how wind and solar plants support the voltage, adjusting their reactive power to the local voltage along a grid-code Q(V) characteristic. This research examines whether grid foundation models, fast neural network models for power-flow analysis, can represent this behaviour. We introduce the Q(V) bus, a new bus type for the open-source GridFM GraphKit, whose physics decoder evaluates the reactive injection from the plant characteristic at the predicted voltage in every correction step. Training data comprise 7882 hourly operating points of a modified IEEE 118-bus system, matched to Energinet DK1/DK2 measurements and solved with pandapower. Compared with a physics-informed baseline that represents the parks as negative load, the Q(V) bus lowers the voltage-magnitude RMSE 2.3-fold, reduces the power-balance mismatch at the parks from 25 to 0.66 MVA, and raises the F1 score for voltage-band violations from 0.40 to 0.76. An ablation over five configurations shows that the Q(V) bus type provides the accuracy and the physics loss provides the power balance. For edge monitoring, we distil the trained $2.0\times10^{7}$-parameter model into students of $6\times10^{5}$ and $1.3\times10^{6}$ parameters; a flat-start initialisation prevents their collapse into an incorrect low-voltage state in which the characteristic saturates. With output distillation, the 12-step student reaches 1.4 times the teacher's error and, for edge inference on an NVIDIA Jetson Orin Nano (15 W), solves an hourly scenario in 12.2 ms, within the 20 ms synchrophasor interval.

</details>

Abstract: We study dedicated actuators installed at distinct states of a structured linear system. Failures remove actuators but preserve states and state arcs. For unit costs and one failure, an exact source-quota relaxation followed by maximum matching in a \emph{common-repair graph} yields a $3/2$-approximation on arbitrary directed state graphs using $O(n^3)$ elementary graph operations. The analysis combines fundamental cocircuit unions with the Tutte--Berge formula. For multiple failures, we give an exact algorithm fixed-parameter tractable in the maximum-matching deficiency and failure budget, independently of the number of source strongly connected components. The underlying result solves resilient matroid spanning with an arbitrary number of disjoint unit-cost quota constraints. We also establish an exact weighted spanning algorithm for safe and vulnerable elements in the independence-oracle model, with an application to strongly connected state graphs.

Abstract: Compositional dissipativity is attractive for large-scale interconnected systems, but its application to modern heterogeneous power systems is limited by conservative descriptions of nodal dissipativity and inefficient distributed implementation. This paper introduces a common first-order dynamic lifting of the physical voltage-current port, providing an enriched dissipativity representation that captures complementary frequency-dependent energy behavior while preserving network composability. The resulting representation accommodates heterogeneous power system buses within a unified dissipativity description and enables less conservative compositional stability analysis. A centralized stability-certification problem is then formulated and solved through a dual-guided column-generation scheme. Its matrix dual reveals the network-critical dissipation direction, which guides parallel local certificate generation toward the current system bottleneck. This leads to a distributed Network-Critical Margin Exchange (NCME) method with early stability certification. Case studies demonstrate substantially reduced conservatism of the dynamic-port formulation and improved computational and communication scalability of NCME for heterogeneous power systems.

</details>

Abstract: Data-driven predictive control (DDPC) has attracted substantial interest, and many variants have been proposed; however, comparatively less is known about the statistical closed-loop performance of these methods and the fundamental limits they are subject to. In this contribution, we apply the statistical decision framework developed in our earlier work to study fundamental limits of DDPC. The performance of a data-driven controller is measured by its risk, the expected deterioration in control performance relative to an oracle model-based controller at a given system, and by its total risk that measures the overall performance across all systems of interest. Lower bounds on these risks are derived using the bias-variance decomposition and the Cramer-Rao inequality. In particular, a lower bound on the total risk is obtained by a calculus-of-variations method. This bound applies to any DDPC design, thereby quantifying unavoidable performance limitations and providing a benchmark for assessing existing DDPC methods. In an illustrative example, we compare the risks of certainty-equivalence model predictive control, subspace predictive control, and data-enabled predictive control with the derived lower bounds, providing a quantitative performance assessment of these methods and some guidance on tuning regularization parameters in DDPC.

</details>

Abstract: Physics-based safety gates for large language model (LLM) agents can approve unsafe actions when the agent and gate rely on the same corrupted measurements or network records. We formulate this shared-evidence failure for voltage-constrained distributed energy resource (DER) export decisions and analyze how nearly critical DER telemetry can bias state estimation while evading residual-based bad-data detection. We propose evidence-independent verification (EIV), which combines advanced metering infrastructure (AMI) voltage readings, measurement selection based on predicted voltage rise, and bounded physical probes. An IEEE 123-bus hidden-truth testbed evaluates 1,654 near-limit requests under telemetry attacks and record errors. For telemetry attacks targeting the nominal gate, an AC-twin gate with bad-data detection approved 95% of unsafe requests, compared with 3.1% for EIV; EIV rejected 22% of safe clean requests. At matched false-rejection settings, direct AMI checks outperformed incorporating the same readings into state estimation. Gate-targeted attacks exposed a vulnerability in telemetry-based response prediction. Command-based prediction removed that route, but targeted attacks on fresh validation cases still produced a 20% unsafe-approval rate under the tested attack pool. LLM agents frequently sought independent evidence voluntarily, but injected instructions and phase-label errors caused unsafe decisions. When offered the EIV checker as a tool, they used it in 93-100% of episodes; mandatory use did not reduce observed violations, which primarily reflected checker errors. These results distinguish enforcement of verification from reliability of the verifier itself.

</details>

Abstract: Lyapunov functions are the cornerstone of stability analysis for dynamical systems, yet synthesizing a symbolic form remains difficult as no universal constructive algorithm exists. To address this challenge, we introduce SLyM, a framework that harnesses the knowledge and reasoning of large language models (LLMs) to discover symbolic Lyapunov functions. To avoid charging the LLM with ill-suited numerical generation, SLyM decomposes the construction into structure proposal and coefficient optimization: the LLM draws on its prior knowledge of Lyapunov constructions to propose the function structure, whose coefficients then follow from a convex program. Local-search structures are folded back into supervision, extending coverage to systems missed by the initial proposer. Across all benchmarks, SLyM consistently outperforms the evaluated neural, sum-of-squares, and generative baselines.

</details>

Abstract: With increasing renewable energy integration, grid-forming (GFM) inverters are becoming key components of future power systems. Compared with synchronous generators (SGs), GFM inverters have limited overcurrent capability and require current limiting. This can lead to switching between normal and current-limited operating modes under large disturbances, complicating synchronization dynamics and posing a longstanding challenge to existing transient-stability analysis methods. In this paper, a state-dependent switched (SdS) energy function theory is proposed for transient-stability analysis of GFM inverters with current limiting, whose dynamics can be abstracted as SdS nonlinear systems. Regional energy functions are constructed for individual operating modes and continuously matched at switching interfaces, yielding a continuous piecewise energy function. Hence, the derived conservative estimate of the stability region (region of attraction, ROA) spans different operating regions without prescribing subsequent current limiting activations and releases, a fundamental distinction from conventional methods using signal-mode energy functions. Techniques for incorporating damping energy are extended to the proposed framework to capture damping effects and further reduce the conservatism of the ROA estimates. The methodology applies to GFM inverters with different synchronization control loops and current-limiting strategies. Experiments on a physical GFM inverter under various grid contingencies validate the proposed quantitative transient stability assessment.

</details>

Abstract: Conventional nonlinear flight controllers operate under the implicit assumption of reliable, high-confidence state feedback. During transient sensing degradation or partial unobservability, state estimation uncertainty is frequently misinterpreted as physical tracking error, triggering aggressive and parasitic corrective inputs. To mitigate this vulnerability, we propose a generalized Estimation-Aware (EA) control framework that directly incorporates real-time estimator confidence into control execution. By mapping the posterior error covariance $\mathbf{P}_{k\mid k}$ into an uncertainty-dependent modulation operator, the EA mechanism dynamically scales feedback authority, performing anisotropic attenuation along weakly observed state directions to prevent false error corrections. Validated across six distinct control paradigms, the proposed approach demonstrates substantial performance gains across all baselines. In particular, when integrated into an Incremental Nonlinear Dynamic Inversion (INDI) architecture, the EA mechanism reduces tracking and estimation errors by 24.34% and 33.24%, respectively, at a high flight speed of 10 m/s.

</details>

Abstract: This paper studies adaptive robust output regulation of linear time-varying systems with unknown constant parameters in both the plant and exosystem. The design is based on nonmonic differential relations for the ideal input. For general time-varying and periodic systems, we derive sufficient conditions for the existence of nonmonic differential relations and construct them from the original system coefficients. Based on these relations, an adaptive controller is constructed using stable filters and a parameterized readout. Under suitable conditions on the filter and readout, the controller achieves exact regulation for finitely separable relation coefficients, including the affine parametric uncertainty treated in Byrnes--Isidori form. For coefficient families satisfying the approximation conditions, refining the approximation achieves any prescribed ultimate regulation accuracy with a fixed stabilization design, yielding practical regulation. Neither persistent excitation nor parameter convergence is required. Two numerical examples illustrate the regulation performance of the proposed controller. Overall, the framework includes earlier constructions based on finite separation of the ideal input or monic linear differential relations with finitely separable coefficients, and achieves exact robust regulation for a broader class of uncertain systems.

</details>

Abstract: We consider controller synthesis for distance-constrained formations, aiming to stabilize the target formation manifold while sustaining admissible equilibrium edge forces. Using the newly introduced notion of transversal passivity, we encode the target formation shape, the prescribed edge lengths, and a subspace of internally balanced edge forces as the port target manifolds of the agents and the edge controller. The synthesis task is thereby reformulated as the joint design of a transversally passive edge controller and its output-target subspace. We then propose a general constructive realization of output-strictly transversally passive systems and specialize it to derive a dynamic edge controller that sustains admissible equilibrium edge forces. Through a compatibility analysis across the interconnection, we establish a closed-loop dissipation margin in terms of the controller parameters and prove local convergence of all agent and controller ports to their target manifolds. Numerical simulations validate the results.

</details>

Abstract: Hosting capacity is commonly assessed with steady-state optimization, which leaves the dynamic response of the data center and the grid at the resulting operating point untested. This paper assesses candidate data center capacities by coupling a unit commitment (UC) model with electromagnetic transient (EMT) simulation. During the capacity search, the UC model determines the economic dispatch for each tested data center capacity, and the resulting operating points are tested for settling with the generators committed online by the UC model and after a selected synchronous generator outage. Three nested capacities are obtained: the optimization-based steady-state capacity, the dynamic pre-outage capacity, and the post-outage dynamic capacity, each bounded by the previous one. On a test system with a detailed data center model, both dynamic capacities fall well below the optimization capacity, and the ranking of connection locations changes with the assessment stage and with the outage. In the most severe outage case at each candidate bus, the capacity is about 30% below the steady-state capacity. Thus, the dynamic response of the data center and the grid should be part of the hosting capacity assessment, and the comparison of connection locations should be made on the post-outage dynamic capacity.

</details>

Abstract: Power system operations rely on approximate solutions of the AC optimal power flow problem, most notably the DC optimal power flow used in security-constrained unit commitment and economic dispatch, while convex relaxations, machine learning surrogates, and other approximations are studied extensively in the academic literature. Because these approximations neglect or relax parts of the AC power flow equations, their solutions must be adjusted before dispatch; for example, the DC optimal power flow, which neglects transmission losses, reactive power, and voltage magnitude variations, is known to produce solutions that are always infeasible. The cost of this adjustment is not adequately understood. This paper proposes a method that quantifies, for any approximate solution, the minimum cost of the adjustments that restore AC feasibility, which is a lower bound on the cost of the heuristic adjustments used in practice. We apply it to the DC optimal power flow, the second-order cone and quadratic convex relaxations, and the AC optimal power flow solved to a loose relative tolerance of $10^{-4}$ instead of $10^{-8}$. Relative to the AC optimal power flow solution obtained from a local nonlinear optimization solver, and even with the minimum adjustment, the DC optimal power flow requires adjusting, on average, generators that account for 15.6% of total generation capacity and incurs a 1.10% cost increase; the convex relaxations require more adjustment (17.2% and 16.9%) but smaller cost increases (0.68% and 0.78%); and the loose-tolerance AC optimal power flow requires 6.1% and incurs 0.42%. These results indicate significant value in operating markets directly on the AC optimal power flow, which avoids the distortion of the dispatch and prices caused by heuristic feasibility restoration.

</details>

This paper presents scalable, vendor-neutral reference electrical architectures for colocation, hyperscale, and AI data centers. Abstract: We propose an adaptive model predictive control (MPC) approach to damp sub-synchronous oscillations (SSOs) induced by grid-following (GFL) inverter-based resources (IBRs). Unlike traditional model predictive controller designed considering the nominal plant that may not perform well in other conditions, we use plants that alter with changing operating point. To capture the plant dynamics, we use recursive least squares (RLS) based system identification with variable forgetting factor. This approach allows us to discount past data and avoid bias caused by large transient outliers while performing system identification. We present a proof of the convergence of the model parameters in the presence of finite outliers. We propose to update the model inside the MPC optimization problem periodically after a predetermined time interval instead of each sampling instant since the operating point changes slowly in a practical power system. The MPC design allows us to directly impose constraints on control inputs so that the IBR current limits are respected. Our approach also addresses the issue of unavailability of IBR models. We show the accuracy of the system identification and the superior performance of the proposed controller on the modified IEEE $4$-machine system and the modified IEEE $68$-bus system as compared with the traditional MPC.

</details>

Abstract: Disturbance uncertainty requires safety filters based on control barrier functions (CBFs) to leave a robustness margin, which can alter the nominal control input and degrade closed-loop performance. This paper studies safety and the resulting performance loss for discrete-time nonlinear systems with additive disturbances and polytopic input constraints. We develop a causal disturbance observer and construct two robust barrier conditions. The first uses a time-dependent bound on the estimation error. The second uses a constant margin when the disturbance increments are uniformly bounded and moves the requirement on the initial error from the online filter to an explicit initialization condition. For affine barriers, both filters are quadratic programs. We bound their input deviations from an oracle filter with access to the current disturbance and derive sufficient conditions for uniform contraction of the oracle closed loop. The contraction analysis accounts for state-dependent actuation, unmatched disturbances, and active physical input limits. Combining the sensitivity and contraction estimates gives finite-time dynamic regret bounds that quantify the effects of observer convergence, disturbance variation, and robustness margins. With sublinear cumulative certified disturbance variation and observer innovation error, the bounds yield sublinear regret.

</details>

Abstract: This paper presents a nonlinear control law for a slung payload system (SPS) consisting of a quadrotor unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) carrying a slung payload. We propose a fault-tolerant control (FTC) design for the case of a complete single-rotor failure, i.e., only three functioning propellers. The failure is not modeled as a disturbance to be handled by estimation. Rather, we model the faulty SPS in control-affine form and design a quasi-static feedback law directly for the faulty dynamics. The controller achieves local exponential convergence of the payload position to a geometric path in space, rather than tracking a timed reference trajectory. Moreover, the transverse dynamics are exponentially stable, and the path-following manifold is rendered controlled invariant by feedback. Controlled invariance allows motion along the path to be prescribed independently through a tangential command while the state stays on the path-following manifold. The UAV body angular velocity remains bounded under bounded applied torque despite the failure. To validate the control design, numerical simulations apply the controller derived from the faulty SPS model in coordinates to the coordinate-free SPS model. Code and animations are available at https://gradslab.github.io/qs-sl/.

</details>

This article investigates the transformative potential of grid-interactive data center microgrids, which integrate battery energy storage, on-site generation, and intelligent microgrid controllers to actively support both facility and grid operations. The proposed simulation framework enables the study comparing conventional and grid-interactive architectures under a range of steady-state and contingency scenarios. Results reveal that grid-interactive microgrids can substantially reduce peak grid demand, improve voltage stability, and improve system reliability especially when data center and system load peaks coincide. Abstract: We demonstrate the use of reinforcement meta-learning (meta-RL) to optimize a target jerk estimation policy, which is then used as the target acceleration rate of change in the propagation model of an unscented Kalman filter (UKF). The policy is learned through simulated engagements of a terminal phase high-altitude endoatmospheric interception of a highly maneuverable target, with a hit-to-kill accuracy requirement. As benchmarks, we tuned both a standard UKF and an interacting multiple model (IMM) filter, where the IMM uses a different constant jerk assumption for each model. The benchmarks and the proposed augmented UKF with the target jerk policy are tested in a 6-DOF simulation environment, which has sufficient fidelity to capture most factors relevant to guidance, navigation, and flight control. The augmented UKF demonstrates significant improvement in target interception rate, with more challenging maneuvers giving the largest performance differential. The meta-RL jerk policy is then tested on an expanded engagement scenario, with increased initial state estimate uncertainty and novel target maneuvers, demonstrating that the meta-RL policy generalizes well to conditions not seen during optimization.

Abstract: Current Model Predictive Control (MPC) implementations are predominantly digital and, despite offering high precision and flexibility, often face challenges with ultra-low latency and hardware certification for safety-critical applications. We present an approach for solving the Quadratic Program (QP) of MPC using a continuous-time neural ordinary differential equation in the dual space. We obtain the solution to these QPs by mapping the differential equation's equilibrium point back to the primal space via a nonlinear transformation. The underlying dual-flow dynamics operate natively on analogue hardware. We validate this approach by demonstrating the hardware implementation of linear MPC on "The Analog Thing" analogue computer. Finally, we extend the framework to nonlinear MPC with successive linearisation, broadening the applicability beyond linear systems.

Abstract: We study networked agents that must agree on a decision minimizing the sum of their local costs, where each cost contains unmeasurable stochastic parameters that evolve with linear but unknown dynamics shared by all agents. Each agent receives only finitely many noisy gradient measurements and must then keep tracking the moving minimizer. Our scheme reconstructs the parameters from windows of gradients, learns the shared dynamics cooperatively by averaging instrumental-variable moment matrices over the network, and tracks the minimizer by gradient tracking on forecast gradients. We bound the error of every agent in learning the dynamics and in tracking the minimizer, and show that the learning error, and through it the tracking error, decreases with the number of agents. We demonstrate the approach on two case studies.

</details>

Abstract: Rapid data-center expansion is raising concerns about electricity affordability, yet how the costs of serving these large new loads reach residential customers remains poorly understood. Here, we quantify the potential impact of 371 announced Texas data-center projects, totaling 38.9 GW by 2030, on residential electricity costs by linking power system expansion, wholesale electricity markets, transmission reinforcement, and retail cost allocation. We find that accommodating this demand increases modeled residential electricity costs in one representative case by 1.64 cents/kWh, with 68% arising from higher wholesale electricity prices and 32% from regulated transmission charges. Allowing new gas generation near data centers reduces the 2023 residential cost increase to 0.92 cents/kWh, whereas excluding data centers from the transmission cost-sharing base raises it to 1.87 cents/kWh. These findings reveal that the residential cost burden of data-center growth is driven primarily by wholesale electricity price increases, rather than direct transmission investment costs, and depends critically on generation siting, infrastructure planning, and cost allocation.

</details>

Abstract: The goal of this paper is to construct open, continental-scale synthetic transmission grids with an optimization method that stays tractable. Security restrictions keep models of the real grid out of open research and machine-learning datasets, and optimization-based construction is hard at scale: the number of candidate lines grows combinatorially, so electrically faithful methods prune the candidates in advance. This paper presents GridForge, which keeps every bus pair within physical length limits as a candidate, selects lines with Garver's length-weighted network-flow linear program instead of binary decisions, and resizes them with a sequential direct-current (DC) power flow. Two stages of 13 regional networks are evaluated. Their node-degree distributions are nearly as strongly log-linear as those of the real Eastern and Western Interconnections ($R^2$ of 0.92 to 0.98 against 0.971 and 0.981), although the areas under the fitted lines differ from the real ones by 39.9% on average. On the upper Midwest and Tennessee Valley footprints, resizing lowers the DC power-flow loss from 5.89% to 4.14% and from 14.55% to 7.05%, and the DC optimal power-flow loss from 4.03% to 2.02% and from 5.54% to 2.47%. Without relaxing any line limit, the DC optimal power flow is feasible in 78.81% and 0.82% of the scenarios. Most lines are built at 69 kV. The models span 250 to 78,484 buses, were built on single machines, and are freely redistributable.

</details>

Abstract: The rapid growth of distributed energy resources (DERs), including rooftop solar and energy storage, is transforming the grid edge, where customer-side assets increasingly interact with the broader power grid. DER aggregators, entities that coordinate the actions of many small-scale DERs, play a key role in this transformation. This paper presents a hybrid Mean-Field Control (MFC) and Mean-Field Game (MFG) framework for integrating DER aggregators into wholesale electricity markets. Unlike traditional approaches that treat market prices as exogenous, our model captures the feedback between aggregators' collective actions and electricity prices. The MFC component optimizes DER operations within each aggregator, while the MFG models strategic interactions among multiple aggregators. To account for various uncertainties, we incorporate reinforcement learning, which allows aggregators to learn optimal bidding strategies in dynamic market conditions. We prove the existence and uniqueness of a mean-field equilibrium and validate the framework through a case study of the Oahu Island power system. Results show that our approach reduces price volatility and improves market efficiency, offering a scalable and decentralized solution for DER integration in wholesale markets.

</details>

Abstract: Victim search in wilderness areas is time-critical. Drones enable rapid area search, but scheduling them is challenging because victims may continue moving, detection results are uncertain, and effective decisions must combine structured mission information with unstructured contextual clues and domain knowledge. To address this, we propose an agent-aided drone scheduling system. The system employs an agent at the ground control station (GCS) to coordinate multiple drones over successive search cycles. In each cycle, the agent applies a scheduling policy that combines a large language model (LLM) for reasoning about where to search with an optimizer for determining how to dispatch the drones under energy constraints. Simulation results show that the proposed system consistently completes the victim-search mission, whereas none of the six non-LLM baselines does so and direct LLM scheduling is considerably less reliable. Abstract: Anesthesia dynamics are commonly described by Wiener models, consisting of linear dynamics followed by a static nonlinearity. This nonlinear output complicates the derivation of theoretical guarantees on closed-loop stability and convergence. Model Predictive Control (MPC) is particularly suitable for anesthesia control, as it explicitly accounts for constraints in the control design. In this paper, we propose an incremental MPC formulation for tracking a desired reference. The proposed approach exploits the structure of the nonlinear output equation to reformulate the control problem within a linear framework. An incremental model is then employed to avoid the explicit computation of steady states and inputs, which may be inaccurate in the presence of model uncertainties. The proposed controller is evaluated through simulations involving 1000 virtual patients and compared with a benchmark MPC formulation. Results show improved steady-state reference tracking while maintaining satisfactory disturbance rejection.

</details>

Abstract: Artificial intelligence data centers (AIDCs) are rapidly increasing in number and capacity. These large electronic loads are interfaced through power electronic devices and can sharply reduce grid power intake during fault-induced voltage sags. Disturbances exceeding equipment ride-through capabilities may also trigger protective disconnection, posing risks to power system security. Static synchronous compensators (STATCOMs) and appropriately controlled AIDC uninterruptible power supply (UPS) rectifiers can provide reactive power support during voltage sags. This paper compares the individual and combined effectiveness of STATCOM and UPS-based voltage support in mitigating fault-induced reductions in grid power intake through electromagnetic transient simulations of a 1 GW AIDC connected to an IEEE nine-bus system. Simulation results show that STATCOM support better preserves short-term intake in milder tested faults, whereas UPS coordination maintains rectifier operation under deeper sags by reducing active intake to reserve reactive current capacity. Combined support improves auxiliary load retention and recovery in selected cases. However, severe tested sags still cause substantial grid-demand reductions even with combined support.

</details>

Abstract: The value of renewable energy devices depends on their operation, which must adapt to weather and demand using limited, imperfect forecasts that update over time. Conventional energy systems planning practices assume unlimited foresight in a single-level formulation, potentially leading to long-term investment in suboptimal device sizes. Accounting for realistic operation corresponds to a bi-level problem: the upper level selects device sizes, while the lower level contains closed-loop operation under evolving forecasts. We solve this problem using backpropagation MPC (BP-MPC), a gradient-based method, with a deterministic MPC as the lower-level controller. Using forecast errors generated by a parameterized martingale model of forecast evolution, we investigate how forecast accuracy affects the benefits of operation-aware energy systems planning and the resulting device sizes. In a case study of an energy system comprising photovoltaics, a battery, a heat pump, and thermal storage with uncertain weather and energy demands, accounting for closed-loop operation yields higher average cost savings as forecast error increases. The resulting designs achieve these savings by progressively shifting investment from battery storage to solar generation and thermal storage, which buffers forecast mismatches.

</details>

Abstract: Classical funnel control keeps the tracking error within a prescribed, time-varying envelope by increasing the feedback gain as the error approaches the boundary. For mechanical systems, this guarantee typically relies on velocity measurements. We show that it can be retained for fully actuated Euler--Lagrange systems using only position measurements. The proposed controller replaces the velocity error with a difference quotient of the position error over a fixed delay, while preserving the classical unbounded gain. It requires no plant model, reference derivatives, observer, or filter states. The central challenge is to reconcile the averaging inherent in delayed feedback with a gain that can grow without bound near the funnel boundary. We resolve this by establishing an a priori bound on the gain along closed-loop trajectories, independent of the delay. This bound yields a computable delay threshold that guarantees tracking within the prescribed funnel and boundedness of all closed-loop signals. The threshold depends only on bounds on the inertia, forces, and reference. Simulations demonstrate tracking accuracy comparable to full-state funnel control and tolerance of delays substantially longer than certified. With quantized position measurements, the controller also requires substantially less torque than an existing derivative-free funnel controller.

</details>

Abstract: Learning the dynamics of mechanical systems subject to nonlinear nonholonomic constraints is challenging, as data-driven models may violate the underlying physical constraints, leading to inconsistent predictions. To address this problem, we develop a constraint-preserving learning framework based on the Lagrange-d'Alembert-Chetaev equations and tangency to the constraint submanifold, which together define an affine space of admissible accelerations. Using the kinetic-energy metric and the Moore-Penrose pseudoinverse, we construct a metric projector and the corresponding Chetaev correction. Embedding this geometric structure directly into a Gaussian process prior ensures that both prior and posterior dynamics satisfy the acceleration constraints exactly. We characterize the resulting reproducing kernel Hilbert space (RKHS), derive an adapted-coordinate representation, and establish nonexpansiveness and consistency transfer. Numerical experiments on a three-dimensional directional flocking system with up to 50 agents demonstrate exact constraint preservation and robustness to constraint-incompatible observations.

</details>

Abstract: Differential flatness enables efficient planning and control, but discovering explicit flat outputs for general nonlinear systems remains difficult due to the lack of general methods and supervised data. By reversing the defining reconstruction of flatness, we generate system-flat-output pairs with known certificates, then fine-tune an LLM to propose symbolic flat-output candidates for unseen dynamics. On literature and synthetic benchmarks, FlatSeek outperforms both the traditional symbolic methods and the untuned reference models in flat-output discovery success, and uncovers alternative symbolic flat outputs not reported in the literature.

Abstract: We develop a distributed quantum network flow for solving linear equations from locally stored row data. Local pumping enforces each node's equation constraint, while neighboring registers interact through swaps. The node marginals obey an exact, closed flow combining consensus with local constraint enforcement. For consistent equations, all node states converge exponentially to a common state supported on the solution space; a unique solution yields full-network convergence to the product solution state. For inconsistent equations of full column rank, scaled encoding and readout recover the least-squares solution through successive large-gain and small-scale limits. For consistent systems, the marginal convergence rate is nondecreasing and concave in the swap gain, approaching a graph-independent limit for fixed local dissipators. Its leading finite-gain correction couples graph topology and row placement through the Laplacian pseudoinverse, enabling semidefinite optimization of edge rates under a fixed total swap-rate budget. For invertible systems, a dissipative HHL-type construction using the same pumping law provides a comparison of solution-state encodings, decay rates, and row-update locality under squared-row-norm pumping. Numerical examples are provided to validate the presented results.

</details>

Abstract: Field demonstrations of advanced heating, ventilation, and air-conditioning control often operate a few thermal zones within a larger building and report savings for those zones alone. A recent theorem showed that this practice overestimates whole-building savings, because heat drawn from the neighboring zones is paid for by their equipment and is not counted. The error grows with the thermal conductance between the controlled and uncontrolled zones, with how much colder the controlled zones run over the test, and with the price of the heat their neighbors supply. For one controlled zone with one neighbor, a single thermal price, and slowly changing prices, the relative error reduces to the ratio of two conductances, a screening number that can be evaluated from the two conductances before a demonstration begins. This paper tests how far that reduction can be taken. A two-story house with one thermal zone per floor is simulated over a 90-day heating season in West Lafayette, Indiana, with receding-horizon model predictive control on the first floor and setpoint control on the second, under four combinations of electricity tariff (time-of-use or flat) and heating plant (heat pump or electric resistance). The exact correction from the theorem recovers the true savings in every case, and the full theorem, evaluated from the controlled zone's temperatures, the prices, and the building parameters, reproduces the measured relative error in every case. The conductance ratio bounds that error from above in all four cases, tightly under a flat tariff, within 3%, and loosely under a time-of-use tariff, where most of the controller's savings come from shifting load through the thermal mass rather than from lowering the temperature.

</details>

Abstract: This paper establishes the relationship between loss-of-load expectation (LOLE) and loss-of-load frequency (LOLF). We show why LOLE generally does not measure distinct-event frequency, mean event duration, or shortage magnitude. We identify conditions under which peak-based LOLE admits a frequency interpretation as the expected count of deficient daily peaks per horizon and distinguish this quantity from LOLF. We also derive an analytical formulation for frequency-and-duration indices that combines a convolution-derived generation probability-and-frequency model with chronological load changes. Under stationary generation, it uses the prescribed load profile directly, without constructing a separate load model or discretizing load magnitudes. Studies using generation and load data from the 24-bus IEEE RTS reveal different event frequencies and mean event durations at identical peak-based LOLE, motivating their separate assessment. Numerical comparisons demonstrate the accuracy advantage of the proposed analytical calculation over the conventional analytical implementations, quantifying the LOLF errors incurred by load discretization.

Abstract: This paper investigates how the dynamic modes of a power system contribute to the stationary variance of its variables under continuous stochastic excitation. Using a modal decomposition of the covariance matrix, the variance of each state or algebraic variable can be resolved into components associated with individual modal responses and their interactions. These components are shown to correspond to within-mode variance and cross-modal covariance, respectively, and the corresponding normalized contributions are termed mode-in-variance participation factors (PFs). The proposed PFs can be used to identify modes governing the variability of important system quantities such as frequency and voltage, quantify the effect of modal interactions, and reveal control dynamics that can be targeted to reduce their stochastic variability. These capabilities are demonstrated through case studies based on the Kundur two-area and IEEE 39-bus systems.

</details>

Abstract: This paper presents improvements to the single-input space vector (SISV) concept for current control in a battery cycler based on a two-phase interleaved buck converter. The cycler is controlled to output a dc-biased sinusoidal current, enabling investigation of second-harmonic ripple effects on lithium-ion cell aging. A modified SISV generation scheme is introduced to track current references containing both dc and sinusoidal components, and an impedance-angle compensation method is proposed to decouple the d and q control axes without disturbing dc tracking. A dq phase-portrait visualization method is also introduced to evaluate transient performance for both SISV and proportional-integral-resonant (PIR) control methods. Simulation and experimental results validate dc-biased sinusoidal current tracking using PIR control and SISV control with different decoupling schemes. The phase-portrait framework allows for straightforward evaluation of transient performance for both SISV and PIR control methods and supports controller tuning to obtain desired trajectories. The results demonstrate the feasibility of SISV-based current control as an alternative to PIR control for battery-cycler applications. The proposed improvements support the development of battery cyclers capable of controlling both dc and sinusoidal current components.

Abstract: Integrated electric vehicle chargers reduce cost by sharing power conversion hardware between the onboard charger (OBC) and the wireless charger (WC). A previously proposed dual active bridge (DAB)-based OBC/WC shares the rectification stage between the OBC and WC; however, the modulation required to realize its wireless operation has not yet been reported. This paper details a modulation strategy that enables the operation of the integrated OBC/WC topology. Differential-mode operation is used for the OBC, and common-mode operation is used for the WC. In OBC mode, the shared full-bridge produces differential-mode voltage for wired power transfer while ideally generating zero common-mode voltage on the wireless charging path. In WC mode, the same full-bridge produces common-mode voltage for wireless power transfer while ideally generating zero differential-mode voltage on the onboard charging path. The method preserves conventional DAB phase-shift power control and enables bidirectional power transfer in wired and wireless charging modes without additional contactors. Design considerations for the OBC transformer and WC coils are derived based on the resulting square-wave voltage amplitudes. Offline PLECS simulations demonstrate power tracking and decoupled charger operation at $\pm$7.4 kW. Hardware-in-the-loop simulations using a PLECS RT Box and an external Texas Instruments microcontroller further validate the proposed modulation method.

</details>

Abstract: When demand exceeds the maximum production capacity of a supply chain, it can be satisfied only for a finite time, determined by the inventory stored along the chain. Existing control methods ensure indefinite constraint satisfaction under bounded demand, but provide no guarantees on how long a supply chain can withstand a shock in which demand goes beyond production capacity. We address this gap for a serial supply chain with transportation delays and flow constraints. Under a specified flow policy, we derive necessary and sufficient conditions on the inventory distribution that guarantee a prescribed lower bound on the exit time from the set where demand is served. We use these conditions to compute inventory targets that minimize holding costs via a quadratic program. Then, we propose a two-mode controller that builds reserves under ordinary demand and depletes them during the shock. Simulations illustrate this approach.

</details>

Abstract: Patient-specific airway modelling requires geometrically consistent lumen surfaces with identifiable boundaries and support for controlled anatomical modifications. We present CT-RespGeomLib, a deterministic framework that converts CT airway masks into two complementary representations: an editable assembly of semantically labelled pipe and junction blocks connected through canonical shared ports, and a fused lumen surface reconstructed from tapered implicit primitives with validated port-to-boundary loop correspondence. The parametric representation further enables localised modelling of airway stenosis and dilation without re-segmentation. Evaluated on 42 reference airway masks from ATM22 (15 cases) and AeroPath (27 cases), the framework produced 37 topologically accepted reconstructions, with a median port-to-loop distance of 0.123 mm across 2174 matched ports. Reconstructed and reference equivalent radii showed strong agreement ($R^2 = 0.976$), with a mean bias of -0.414 mm arising primarily from the circular, inscribed-radius representation. Controlled pathology modelling generated 40 variants across five patient-specific geometries, including 25-75% stenosis and 50% dilation at tracheal and main-bronchus locations. Prescribed radius changes were reproduced with a mean absolute ratio error of 0.0056, while the median 95th-percentile deviation outside the modified region was 0.111 mm. Downstream applicability was demonstrated through volume meshing and an OpenFOAM simulation with individually prescribed model-based resistance conditions at 62 outlets. Although additional surface preparation may be required for numerical meshing, CT-RespGeomLib provides a reproducible framework for CT-derived airway reconstruction, quantitative boundary validation and controlled geometric pathology modelling.

</details>

Abstract: Nonlinear time-optimal model predictive control (TOMPC) enables highly performant control of constrained systems, but its computational cost prohibits real-time use on fast dynamical systems. Neural network imitation of the TOMPC control law can reduce online computational costs, yet existing work mostly targets quadratic cost formulations in simulation and addresses the challenges of neural controllers in isolation. This work imitates a long-horizon nonconvex TOMPC with hard terminal constraints, whose high computational cost limits training-data generation. We present (i) \emph{self-informed sampling (SIFS)}, a data-efficient expert-sampling strategy that uses a learned time-to-go to query the expert only where the policy deviates from time-optimal behavior, (ii) a complete training and control architecture that jointly addresses dataset correlation, suboptimal expert labels, covariate shift, constraint satisfaction and model mismatch, and (iii) real-world validation on a Furuta pendulum. SIFS matches standard dataset aggregation performance in roughly one-third of the training time and with less than half the training data. The resulting controller matches the region of attraction of the expert TOMPC while raising the control frequency from 1.4~Hz to 1~kHz. On hardware, the controller swings up and stabilizes the pendulum along trajectories closely matching the expert TOMPC solution.

</details>

Abstract: While conventional optimal control selects an optimal input sequence, probabilistic control assigns probability mass over all possible inputs and exploits a distribution of control sequences. Control distributions help enable exploration, capture diverse solutions, and solve deterministic optimal control problems. This work reviews an important probabilistic model for control distributions, the control-Gibbs distribution, i.e., the Gibbs distribution with an optimal control objective as the energy function. We point out its fundamental role in various probabilistic control frameworks, e.g., maximum entropy control, control-as-inference framework, model predictive path integral control, and energy-based models for control. This provides a model-central perspective to unify these probabilistic control frameworks. We also review the associated extensions of dynamic programming for computing closed-loop control distributions. To sample the control-Gibbs distribution, we consider projected Langevin sampling and develop non-asymptotic guarantees. The guarantees show the dual benefits of sampling the control-Gibbs distribution: obtaining diverse control solutions and solving the deterministic optimal control problem. We provide a numerical example where diverse arm trajectories are sampled from a control-Gibbs distribution.

</details>

Abstract: Sampling-time jitter makes a sampled-data control loop time-varying, so standard LTI robust-control synthesis does not apply directly. This paper reformulates dynamic output feedback design under bounded, unmeasured sample-to-sample jitter as a robust-control problem. The exact zero-order-hold model is decomposed into a nominal time-invariant plant and a memoryless, time-varying uncertainty block with a norm bound representing the sampling-time jitter. As the block is memoryless, the operator small-gain condition for the time varying loop reduces to a standard robustness test with a matrix norm bound. It is shown that the same test also guarantees uniform exponential stability for every admissible jitter sequence. Embedding the decomposition into a generalized plant with performance channels serves two synthesis objectives with standard robust-control tools: Robust stability with optimized nominal performance through structured H-inf synthesis, and robust performance through mu synthesis with constant scalings. A numerical example shows that jitter can destabilize a control loop with a nominal H-inf controller, whereas both robust designs remain stable and the robust-performance design is less sensitive to jitter at the cost of nominal performance.

</details>

Abstract: The increasing penetration of inverter-based resources and growing cyber-physical interdependencies expose electrical power systems to disturbances that challenge conventional resilience assessments. Existing approaches often evaluate resilience dimensions independently, overlooking the non-additive effects of coordinated disruptions and the influence of exogenous stressors. To address this gap, this paper proposes a Grid Multidimensional Resilience Index (GRID-MRI) that integrates endogenous disruption severity, coordination effects, and climatic and economic-regulatory conditions. The framework quantifies physical, cyber, and operational degradation, identifies interaction effects through counterfactual (i.e., what-if), simulations of individual and coordinated attacks, and incorporates exogenous stressors as scenario-dependent scaling factors. Its application to a modified IEEE 39-bus system under three escalating cyber-physical attack scenarios, informed by the December 2025 Polish cyberattack incident, reveals that coordinated attacks induce system collapse despite stable individual and additive responses. The corresponding resilience losses are 3.72 and 4.09 times their additive estimates, while GRID-MRI increases from 0.062 to 1.188 and reaches 6.081 under additional exogenous stress. These findings demonstrate the limitations of additive assessments and establish a quantitative basis for distinguishing the dominant mechanisms of multidimensional resilience degradation.

</details>

Abstract: This paper investigates total energy shaping of underactuated mechanical systems with gravity compensation evaluated at the desired equilibrium instead of throughout the online closed-loop operation. Within the interconnection and damping assignment passivity-based control (IDA-PBC) framework, sufficient conditions for local asymptotic stability are established in terms of integrability of a vector field and positive definiteness of an augmented potential energy. The approach is also combined with a reformulation of potential energy matching that replaces a nonhomogeneous partial differential equation with a homogeneous one and introduces an additional design term. Analytic constructions for the TORA system and a class of two-degree-of-freedom systems illustrate how the matching and integrability requirements can be addressed. The proposed control law avoids online evaluation of the configuration-dependent gravity term; offline controller synthesis and verification can still require knowledge of the gravity model.

Abstract: Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) and Gated Recurrent Unit (GRU) networks are the two most widely adopted recurrent neural networks for black-box nonlinear system identification, yet they are invariably treated as mutually exclusive structural choices. To move toward a general framework that inherits the properties of both networks, we introduce the Generalized Gated Recurrent Neural Network (GGRNN), a unified architecture that embeds the exact GRU and LSTM network dynamics through homotopies between their respective gating mechanisms. Each homotopy continuously deforms one mechanism into the other, with a vector of homotopy parameters blending the contribution of the GRU and LSTM counterparts. Since benchmark comparisons report no consistent winner between the two architectures, the gating mechanism best suited to a given system is unlikely to coincide with either endpoint. We thus let each recurrent unit learn its own position along these homotopies, obtaining gating behaviors that neither endpoint can express. We pair the GGRNN with a two-stage training methodology: in Stage 1 we co-train all shared and network-specific parameters in parallel at the GRU and LSTM endpoint architectures, while in Stage 2 we freeze the network parameters and optimize only the homotopy parameters. Experiments on the Wiener-Hammerstein and Nano-Drone benchmarks show improved simulation accuracy over the co-trained GRU and LSTM networks on most output groups, and over the reference black-box baselines in nearly all comparisons.

</details>

Abstract: This paper studies the problem of data-driven covariance steering for stochastic, nonlinear dynamical systems. We utilize Koopman operator theory to learn, directly from data, an approximate linear representation of the nonlinear dynamics in a lifted space of observable functions of the state. By exploiting this linear model, we design controllers for the mean and covariance of the underlying nonlinear system by utilizing linear distribution steering algorithms. To account for approximation errors in the learned dynamics and the ambiguity in the disturbance distribution induced in the lifted space, we formulate the problem as a distributionally robust covariance steering problem. Furthermore, we leverage a deep neural network (DNN) to simultaneously learn the Koopman observables and the linear model parameters, thus eliminating the need for manual observables basis selection while also reducing the dimensionality of the lifted representation. Additionally, we can promote desirable model properties, such as stability, during DNN training. We demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework via numerical simulations.

</details>

### [524] [One Block Is Enough: LoopMR for Accelerated MRI Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14947)
*Yimeng He,Yibin Xie,Yi Zheng,Yifan Gao,Lixia Wang,Yin-Chen Hsu,Yufeng Wang,Meng Lu,Debiao Li,Xiuzhen Huang*

Main category: eess.IV

TL;DR: 解析错误


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解析错误

Method: 解析错误

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Deep-learning unrolled networks are the leading approach to accelerated MRI reconstruction. State-of-the-art models such as PromptMR+ learn a distinct block for every cascade, so their size grows with the number of cascades. Sharing one block across iterations avoids adding parameters, but earlier shared-weight models typically used a tiny block and produced worse images than models with distinct weights. Therefore, it remained unclear whether weight sharing itself or the choice of block was the problem. Recent advances in looped transformers, which reuse the same block across layers, show that they can match or outperform their distinct-weight counterparts on several tasks. Motivated by this, we propose LoopMR, which reuses a single Restormer block at every iteration as its core. Besides the current image estimate, the block receives data-consistency inputs and latent images. On four datasets covering the brain (CC359), knee (fastMRI), heart (CMRxRecon2023) and carotid (in-house), LoopMR significantly outperforms E2E-VarNet, PromptMR and PromptMR+ in PSNR and SSIM at every acceleration factor (p < 0.01), with about 1/9 of PromptMR+'s parameters (10.3M versus 92.9M). It also keeps its lead at unseen acceleration factors and on an unseen dataset. With our transformer block, the shared model matches or outperforms its distinct-weight counterpart at 6, 12 and 24 iterations. At a fixed number of iterations, shared transformer blocks match or outperform their distinct-weight versions, while shared CNN blocks perform worse. These results suggest that looping one well-chosen block can outperform stacking distinct cascades in MRI reconstruction, with a fraction of the parameters.

</details>


### [525] [Aggregate Accuracy Hides Diagnosis-Specific Failure in Medical Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14975)
*Shreya Ajay Kale,Angad Ripudaman Singh Bajwa*

Main category: eess.IV

TL;DR: 解析错误


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解析错误

Method: 解析错误

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Medical vision-language models (VLMs) are compared mainly by accuracy averaged across findings, a summary that can hide complete failure on a single diagnosis. We audit five open medical VLMs (MedGemma-4B, Lingshu-7B, HuatuoGPT-Vision-7B, Qwen2.5-VL-7B, and MedVLM-R1) on 6,048 ChestX-ray14 questions balanced by sex, age, finding, and label. On the 432 pneumothorax-positive X-rays, HuatuoGPT-Vision and Qwen2.5-VL detect zero cases while MedGemma and Lingshu detect 38% and 67% of the same images. The two failing models answer "no" to all 864 pneumothorax cases (balanced accuracy 0.500, chance), yet produce fluent, anatomically specific explanations that name the signs of a collapsed lung and then deny them, with no hedge or abstention. This failure is not an artifact of prompting, image resolution, label ambiguity, or model scale. It persists across four prompt formats, at native resolution, and on single-label cases. A within-model control shows the same forced prompt gives HuatuoGPT 79% recall on nodule but only 6% on pneumothorax. It survives a tenfold scale increase (Qwen2.5-VL-72B detects 4%) and replicates on SIIM-ACR, an independent dataset with radiologist-drawn masks, where the failing models detect 0 of 432 confirmed pneumothoraces, including 0 of the 144 largest lesions. Reading the models' logits and internal states shows this is not blindness. The two failing models are highly confident yet no better than chance and a linear probe recovers pneumothorax from their hidden representations even where recall is zero, so the finding is represented internally but suppressed at the output. Aggregate accuracy therefore cannot establish that a medical VLM can see a given finding, because a model that misses one can still describe it convincingly. Reporting per-finding recall and specificity on balanced cases is needed to tell the difference.

</details>


### [526] [Coarse-to-Fine 3D MRI Reconstruction via Resolution-Agnostic Neural Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14979)
*Jiayun Wang,Ram Daftari,Weijie Gan,Valentin Duruisseaux,Ruibo Wang,Armeet Singh Jatyani,Boris Bonev,Anima Anandkumar*

Main category: eess.IV

TL;DR: 解析错误


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解析错误

Method: 解析错误

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Deep-learning-based 3D MRI reconstruction is limited in practice by the GPU memory and compute cost of full-volume training; 3D acquisitions are thus most often reconstructed by 2D models applied slice-by-slice and then stacked, which ignores through-plane (stacking-axis) structure. We reconstruct in full 3D and ask whether a model can be trained at low resolution (fast and memory-feasible) yet deployed at higher resolution. This coarse-to-fine strategy needs reliable cross-resolution performance, but standard networks are resolution-specific and degrade substantially when evaluated outside the training setting. We therefore use resolution-agnostic neural operators (NOs) and propose KIR-NO (k-space-to-Image Reconstruction Neural Operator), a discretization-agnostic framework for accelerated 3D MRI reconstruction. KIR-NO is trained with full backpropagation on low-resolution volumes and applied zero-shot to higher-resolution volumes at inference. Its core building block is a 3D neural operator based on discrete-continuous convolutions: convolutional filters are parameterized in a continuous basis and sampled at arbitrary voxel resolutions while preserving the local inductive bias of convolution. KIR-NO integrates these learned operators with physics-based consistency: a k-space neural-operator head first refines the undersampled Fourier measurements, and image-space neural-operator cascades then refine the reconstruction through interleaved data-consistency updates. Across 3D knee and brain MRI benchmarks, KIR-NO improves over classical and CNN baselines: it improves over a state-of-the-art 3D CNN by 2.2 dB PSNR at half resolution under 4x acceleration on SKM-TEA, with gains persisting at 8x and 16x. These results show that resolution-agnostic operators make coarse-to-fine 3D MRI reconstruction - cheap low-resolution training with high-resolution deployment - practical across anatomies.

</details>


### [527] [Interpreting Selective-Review Coverage for Head-and-Neck Auto-Contours](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15255)
*Jaden Moon,Caiwen Jiang,Songlin Zhao,Wei Liu*

Main category: eess.IV

TL;DR: 解析错误


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解析错误

Method: 解析错误

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Selective-review systems aim to reduce human review by passing candidates predicted to be low risk. However, coverage (the fraction passed) can be misleading when measurements needed to interpret risk are unavailable. We separate structural evaluability (a nonempty mask with all required measurements available), computational-endpoint status, and pass-or-review decisions using archived Mayo records and controlled OpenKBP analyses. In 3,968 Mayo records, all 1,167 recorded empty predictions (predicted target-organ masks containing zero voxels despite nonempty reference masks) were endpoint-negative and passed both modules, accounting for approximately 76% of each pass set. In a held-out OpenKBP test, a logistic-regression router achieved an area under the receiver operating characteristic curve of 0.833 and average precision of 0.449 but passed all 544 non-evaluable candidates, including 542 prespecified empty-mask controls and 2 erosions that became empty. A structural gate removed those passes without changing scores or the threshold, preserved all evaluable passes, and assigned 98.71% of endpoint-positive candidates to review. Under the original preprocessing and in patient-disjoint development evaluation, a router trained only on evaluable candidates and a router modeling an unavailable state likewise passed no non-evaluable candidates, with similar evaluable discrimination and at least 99.0% of endpoint-positive candidates assigned to review in pooled outer-fold predictions. These results show that coverage should be interpreted jointly with candidate eligibility, endpoint scope, missingness handling, and routing rules; they do not establish clinical validity, deployment safety, or natural-failure prevalence.

</details>


### [528] [Supervision-Aware Evaluation of MedGemma for Reference-Assisted Radiotherapy Contour Ranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15370)

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Radiotherapy contour ranking can support quality assurance, but vision-language model (VLM) gains may reflect supervision, score construction, available inputs, or representation access. We evaluated frozen MedGemma 1.5 in a single-institution study, a 12-patient held-out HaN-Seg test, five-fold cross-fitting on 30 non-test HaN-Seg patients, and transfer without refitting to 120 SegRap2023 patients. On held-out HaN-Seg, a supervised readout of the final prompt state exceeded a supervised readout of the model's yes/no output probabilities by +0.3047 pooled average precision (AP; 95% patient-bootstrap interval [+0.2822, +0.3269]); the advantage persisted against a parameter-count-matched nonlinear control. A readout of projected visual tokens exceeded the final-state readout in cross-fitting and transfer (+0.0706 AP averaged equally across patients on SegRap; 95% interval [+0.0607, +0.0804]), while a structured panel-feature readout remained competitive with visual tokens. The anatomy-conditioned readout did not demonstrate an advantage over the native-feature comparator. These results motivate visual-token and structured-feature controls in medical-imaging VLM evaluations; outcomes were computational or synthetic, not clinical acceptability.

</details>


### [529] [HR-NVC++: Unified Ultra-High-Resolution Neural Video Coding with Confidence-Guided Spatial-Temporal Cues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15670)
*Feng Ye,Lijiong Chen,Kai Zhang,Li Zhang,Chuanmin Jia*

Main category: eess.IV

TL;DR: 解析错误


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解析错误

Method: 解析错误

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Ultra-high-resolution neural video coding (NVC) remains challenging because temporal correspondence becomes increasingly unreliable as spatial resolution increases. Although recent neural B-frame codecs have improved bi-directional temporal prediction, complex motion and fine-grained structures still lead to unreliable temporal references, while spatial information of the current frame remains underexploited. In this work, we propose HR-NVC++, a unified ultra-high-resolution NVC approach that supports both fidelity-oriented and perceptual-quality-oriented compression with a shared spatial-temporal context. The context integrates bi-directional temporal cues with complementary spatial information: confidence-guided fusion adaptively balances the forward and backward temporal cues according to their estimated correspondence reliability, while a spatial complement branch enriches regions that temporal references fail to explain. The resulting context serves as a common basis for two compression modes. Built upon this shared context, the fidelity mode retains entropy-based latent coding for pixel-accurate compression, whereas the perceptual mode removes additional latent coding and performs reconstruction directly from the context. This design demonstrates that a sufficiently informative spatial-temporal context already carries the dominant content required for perceptually favorable reconstruction, allowing different compression objectives to share the same contextual representation. Extensive experiments on UVG-4K and JVET Class A1/A2 show that HR-NVC++ achieves an average PSNR BD-rate saving of 13.01% over the H.266/VVC reference model VTM-13.2 under the random access configuration, while delivering substantially better perceptual quality and temporal consistency than existing neural video codecs.

</details>


### [530] [Physics-based simulation unlocks scaling laws for generalizable MRI reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15758)
*Hong Shang,Zijian Zhan,Sen Jia,Xiang Fan,Jintao Meng,Yang Zhou,Xiang Zhou,Tianyu He,Xinran Chen,Qiaoling Zeng,Keyan Yu,Jianhua Yao,Dong Liang*

Main category: eess.IV

TL;DR: 解析错误


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解析错误

Method: 解析错误

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Deep-learning-based magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) reconstruction holds great promise for accelerating acquisitions, yet its clinical translation remains bottlenecked by poor generalizability across unseen scanners, populations, anatomies, and protocols. Gathering sufficiently diverse clinical datasets to overcome this bottleneck is hindered by high acquisition costs, privacy restrictions, and institutional biases. Here, we introduce Scalable Inverse Model (SIM), a framework that systematically co-designs physics-simulated training data, model architecture, and learning paradigms around scalability--leveraging compute scaling to overcome historical data bottlenecks and achieve robust zero-shot MRI reconstruction. Across 14 public and in-house datasets--spanning seven international centers, five vendors, field strengths of 0.3-7 T, over ten anatomical regions, more than ten acquisition protocols, downstream quantitative and molecular tasks, and a 150-scan blinded radiologist study--SIM demonstrates robust zero-shot generalizability, recovering fine anatomical details while suppressing aliasing artifacts where conventional deep-learning methods fail. Attention-map analyses confirm that SIM learns physically explainable, dynamically adaptive reconstruction primitives rather than anatomy-specific priors. SIM establishes a scalable, privacy-preserving, and democratized infrastructure for universal MRI reconstruction, providing a blueprint for generalizable AI across inverse problems in medical imaging.

</details>


### [531] [FGCARL: Fisher-Guided Continuously Adaptive Representation Learning for Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16210)
*Xiaobing Yu,Jin Yang,Peijie Qiu,Weiwei Ma,Xiaofeng Liu*

Main category: eess.IV

TL;DR: 解析错误


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解析错误

Method: 解析错误

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Deep learning (DL) has achieved remarkable success in medical image segmentation by learning data-driven representations from large-scale annotated datasets. However, medical images are often collected independently across clinical sites, resulting in substantial domain shifts due to differences in imaging modalities, acquisition protocols, and intensity distributions. Meanwhile, segmentation classes may vary across sites due to differences in annotation standards, leading to class shifts. These evolving domains and class definitions can substantially degrade segmentation performance on newly encountered data, while sequential adaptation may cause catastrophic forgetting of knowledge acquired from previously encountered domains and classes. Existing incremental learning methods for medical image segmentation primarily address either domain shifts or class shifts, with these two settings largely investigated independently. This separation leaves a critical gap in developing a unified approach that can simultaneously adapt to evolving domains and newly introduced classes while retaining previously acquired knowledge. To address this challenge, we propose Fisher-Guided Continuously Adaptive Representation Learning, a unified framework for domain- and class-incremental 3D volumetric medical image segmentation. FGCARL comprises two key components: Mutual Adaptive Instance Representation (MAIR), which adaptively aligns feature distributions across sequentially encountered domains through bidirectional normalization and denormalization, and Fisher-Guided Knowledge Retention (FGKR), which selectively preserves parameters important for previously learned domains and classes through Fisher-guided regularization. Experimental results demonstrate that FGCARL consistently outperformed state-of-the-art methods, effectively adapting to domain and class shifts while mitigating catastrophic forgetting.

</details>


### [532] [CODE-PIF: Convex/Deep (CODE) Multiview Pseudoinvariant Features for Multitemporal Multispectral Surface Reflectance Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16802)
*Chia-Hsiang Lin,Lino Garda Denaro,Jian-Kai Huang*

Main category: eess.IV

TL;DR: 解析错误


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解析错误

Method: 解析错误

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Multitemporal multispectral surface reflectance imagery exhibits radiometric and spectral variability due to atmospheric effects, sensor differences, and actual surface changes, complicating the identification of pseudoinvariant features (PIFs). The SOTA DeepMGCCA captures nonlinear dependencies across acquisition dates, but similarity in its learned feature space does not directly establish consistency in the original reflectance domain. Meanwhile, we believe that the PIFs extracted by DeepMGCCA preserve essential structural characteristics, providing an effective prior to guide subsequent refinement. Therefore, we propose the CODE-PIF architecture, an unsupervised convex/deep (CODE) framework that refines the coarse PIF from DeepMGCCA using convex optimization. Specifically, CODE-PIF uses the data-fitting term to integrate reflectance deviations, correlation-weighted spectral angles, and temporal reference change information to promote radiometric and spectral consistency between predicted PIFs and observed data. Moreover, the regularization term uses a mathematically simple Q-quadratic norm to incorporate information from the coarse PIF estimate into the refined solution. The resulting convex optimization problem is solved using the ADMM optimizer, which decomposes it into simpler subproblems and provides convergence guarantees under standard assumptions. Experiments across four distinct ROIs show improved average radiometric and spectral agreement relative to DeepMGCCA.Averaged across these regions, the RMSE decreases from 0.0192 to 0.0164, PSNR increases from 35.91 to 37.44 dB, and the between-date spectral angle decreases from 3.77 to 2.97 degrees. Although structural similarity and Pearson correlation do not improve in every scene, the results suggest that using observed data helps select PIFs with more consistent reflectance values and spectral shapes across dates.

</details>


### [533] [Transmission-Mode Multispectral Imaging for Estimation of Microcystis spp. Cell Concentration and ELISA-Equivalent Microcystin Concentration in Water](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16859)
*J. A. S. T. Jayakody,G. Thilakarathne,G. A. S. L. Ranasinghe,M. C. L. De Silva,K. A. D. S. A. Perera,G. M. R. I. Godaliyadda,H. M. V. R. Herath,M. P. B. Ekanayake,A. D. S. N. P. Athukorala,H. A. D. R. P. Sirimanna*

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Harmful algal blooms dominated by Microcystis spp. threaten freshwater systems through proliferation and microcystin (MC) production. Conventional measurements of cyanobacterial abundance and MC concentration commonly require laboratory processing, specialized instrumentation, or assay reagents. Because MC concentration cannot always be reliably inferred from cell abundance, complementary optical approaches for estimating both quantities are of interest. This study proposes and evaluates a transmission mode multispectral imaging (MSI) framework for estimating Microcystis spp. cell concentration and ELISA-equivalent MC concentration from culture derived dilution samples. A custom built MSI system acquiring twelve spectral bands from near-ultraviolet to near-infrared wavelengths measured transmitted light spectral responses. Following principal component analysis based dimensionality reduction, spectral variation was quantified using an aggregated Wasserstein distance descriptor for regression based prediction. Cell concentration estimation yielded $R^2=0.9819$ for calibration and $R^2=0.9754$ on held-out intermediate dilution levels, while ELISA-equivalent MC estimation yielded $R^2=0.9788$ for calibration and $R^2=0.9786$ on held-out intermediate dilution levels. These results demonstrate the feasibility of transmission-mode MSI as a dual output optical calibration framework for estimation of Microcystis spp. cell concentration and ELISA-equivalent MC concentration in water.

</details>

Abstract: This document describes open Electrical Impedance Tomography (EIT) measurement data that were collected from biomedical imaging inspired phantoms with moderate conductivity contrasts. The data were collected with the KIT4 and KIT5 EIT measurement devices at the University of Eastern Finland, and are intended for EIT image reconstruction algorithm development and testing. The data from thoracic imaging inspired cylindrical and chest-shaped phantoms are suitable for 2D algorithms, and the data from an intracerebral hemorrhage imaging inspired head-shaped phantom with resistive skull are suitable for 3D algorithms. In addition to the measurement data, computational meshes and example reconstruction codes are provided and sample reconstructions included.
